# Phase B register (2026-09-09)

Produced by nine parallel read-only audits of the spec (sections 3.2, 6, 9, 12, 13, 15, 16, 17, 20, 21, 22, 23, 26, 27), the plan files 07 and 08, the feature specs and the tree at `10650da`. Statuses are as found; `blocked-needs-owner-decision` rows need the owner, `infeasible-explain` rows carry the reason in the work column. The INFRA scope is executed through `docs/plans/10-remaining-work-brief.md` (package E), not the Phase B waves; it is kept here for completeness. The execution waves are in `docs/plans/12-phase-b-plan.md`.

Status (2026-09-09, after Phase B waves B0 to B4). The rows below are kept as found at audit time; closure is recorded in the plan's status lines, master plan section 4.1 and the tree. ## ENDPOINT

ENDPOINT — black-box API endpoint connector: Target.kind=ml_model_endpoint, AuthProfile credentials, query-only access, egress allowlist and rate limits, black-box attacks (HopSkipJump image, ZOO tabular) and model-agnostic SHAP, connection test at registration, the JSON predict-proba contract, a tiny local inference server for the e2e test, audit events and RBAC. Audited read-only on worktree "/Users/noah.behrick/NDIA Hackathon/wt/planb" at 10650da.

| Id | Requirement | Status | Size | Work | Test |
|---|---|---|---|---|---|
| ENDPOINT-01 | POST /v1/models with source="endpoint" registers a Target of kind ml_model_endpoint (value = inference URL) with static checks only in the API: URL shape (https, or plaintext http only for loopback/allowlisted literal-IP hosts; no userinfo, no query string, no fragment), host passes config.target_allowlist through authorize("model.register", target=url), auth_profile_id names an AuthProfile of the same project with kind in {bearer, header} (form/cookie refused), modality in {image, tabular}, dataset_id binds to a bundled evaluation split (check_upload_dataset), class_names/n_classes and input_shape declared, license/ownership statement and a non-mission-system evaluation-instance attestation required. Creates the Target (detail = MLModelManifest with format="endpoint", status registered->validating), an ml.ingest Run and a model.validate Job; returns 201 with status validating. | ENDPOINT-02 | The endpoint request/response contract the connector accepts is specified, versioned and documented: redsim-predict-proba/1. Any other shape, status, or a response > 16 MiB is a contract violation (EndpointSchemaMismatch), never coerced. | ENDPOINT-05 | Endpoint queries are made only by the worker parent, never by the sandbox child and never by the API: a `PredictBroker` runs in the Celery worker process for the duration of an endpoint job, listens on a unix-domain socket inside the 0700 per-job work dir, decrypts the AuthProfile via services.auth_profiles.resolve_auth_for_scan immediately before the job and holds the secret only in memory, opens one httpx.Client with verify from redsim.llm.pythia.tls_verify() (truststore/CA bundle, no verify=False switch) and a bounded timeout, and serves length-prefixed npz frames from the child: {op: predict, x} -> {probabilities} or {error_class, message}. The child’s environment is unchanged (no proxy vars, no credential, no REDSIM_* secret; only the socket path is added to the request JSON). The broker enforces egress checks at connect time (ENDPOINT-07), the rate limit and query budget (ENDPOINT-08), counts rows/requests/bytes per purpose (attack, explain, probe), and closes the socket when the child exits or is killed. This is how the section 9 sandbox rules apply to the new kind. | missing | L | New redsim/ml/endpoint_broker.py: `PredictBroker(url, auth, *, egress: EgressPolicy, limits: EndpointLimits, work_dir, purpose_counter)` with `start() -> socket_path`, `stop() -> BrokerStats`, a ThreadingUnixStreamServer handling frames from redsim/ml/targets/endpoint.py::SocketPredictTransport; `HttpPredictTransport` (used by the broker and, in tests, directly) that encodes per endpoint_contract, retries idempotent predicts up to 2 times on 5xx/timeouts with backoff (counted), and raises typed EndpointUnreachable / EndpointAuthFailed / EndpointSchemaMismatch / EgressRefused / QueryBudgetExceeded (new classes in redsim/ml/errors.py with stable codes). **Recommendation.** Leanest honest path: keep the API static and the sandbox child credential- and network-free by putting the only outbound HTTP in a worker-parent PredictBroker (ENDPOINT-05) that the child reaches over a unix socket in the 0700 work dir; the child runs an ordinary `EndpointTarget` (ENDPOINT-04) whose art_classifier() is ART's BlackBoxClassifier, so the existing campaign runner, HopSkipJump adapter, PartitionExplainer/KernelExplainer paths, scoring, findings, verify loop and reports work without new evidence code. Order: ENDPOINT-03 (one additive `MLModelManifest.endpoint` field, announced per plan 01 §8) -> 02/06/07 (contract, error codes, egress policy; all stdlib+pydantic so the API no-ML test stays green) -> 04 -> 05 (+08 limits) -> 09 (probe as the endpoint variant of model.validate) -> 11/10 (admission + task; land together or behind a REDSIM_ML_ENDPOINT_CONNECTOR flag that keeps 501 until both merge) -> 21/22/23 (TinyTarget http.server fixture, real-child broker test, 3.13-safe route tests) -> 17/18/19/20/29/30/15/27 (projections, delete, audit/redaction, RBAC reorder, Job.detail id, failure semantics, limitations, fingerprint) -> 12/14/16 (image HSJ test, explainer caps, defense wrapping) -> 13 (ZOO, coordinate ownership with the ATTACKS scope) -> 24/25 (terraform scans-SG egress variable for William; docs). ENDPOINT-26 (ownership verification) needs an owner decision; recommend accepting allowlist + admin RBAC + audited attestation (ENDPOINT-31) for the hackathon and recording the divergence. Libraries: nothing new — httpx (already in api and ml extras) with redsim.llm.pythia.tls_verify for truststore, ART BlackBoxClassifier/HopSkipJump/ZooAttack, shap Partition/Kernel, stdlib http.server/socketserver for the test server and the `redsim ml serve` demo endpoint. Datasets: none new — endpoint targets bind to the existing bundled evaluation splits (vehicles imagery, malicious-URLs lexical features; CIFAR-10/TinyTarget only in tests), so no additions to the public data repo are required. Honesty constraints carried: white-box attacks refused with 422 attack_requires_gradients rather than substituted; query counts, rate limits and budgets recorded on every row; transport failures are typed job failures with partial evidence, never model outcomes; remote model identity is labelled through a response fingerprint; the MRI stays per campaign; no LLM is involved anywhere in this scope (Pythia untouched, garak and LLM stub belong to another scope); no UI work.

## LLM

LLM — LLM-domain red-teaming through Pythia with garak 0.16.0 (spec 3.2 bullet 6, 6.6, 11.6, 17.4, 23 B1+, D5/D6/D9): LLM target kind carrying a canonical <vendor>/<model> id, probe task on the default worker pool, garak OpenAICompatible generator pointed at PYTHIA_BASE_URL with the pk_ key and X-Pythia-Persona header, curated probe selection and detectors, per-target probe scorecard (k/n per probe family, never an MRI input), findings projection, report sections, audit vocabulary llm.probe.*, budgets and REDSIM_DISABLE_LLM, per-project limits, the "Don't read the prompts" rule, offline test harness. Web UI excluded. Audited read-only on worktree /Users/noah.behrick/NDIA Hackathon/wt/planb at 10650da.

**Recommendation.** Leanest honest path: build the LLM track as a self-contained package `redsim/ml/llm/` (schema, catalog + committed catalog.json, generator, probe_child, runner, scorecard, findings, rules, reporting) plus one Celery task on the existing `default` queue, one admission service, and four API surfaces (`POST /v1/models source=endpoint endpoint_kind=llm`, `GET /v1/llm/probes`, `POST /v1/models/{id}/probes`, `GET /v1/runs/{id}/llm-scorecard`), with no new Alembic migration (Target.kind/detail, Job.type, Run.scanner, Artifact.kind and findings.schema_blob are all free-form today) and no edit to the frozen `redsim/ml/schema.py` beyond the optional additive `RunSummary.kind/probe_ids` announced in master plan §5; the only frozen-contract touch is one additive `Action.LLM_PROBE_RUN` (remediator). Libraries: garak pinned `>=0.16,<0.17` in the worker image only (plus `libmagic1`), openai 2.x and httpx as garak's transport (the redsim `PythiaGenerator(OpenAICompatible)` subclass supplies `X-Pythia-Persona` via `default_headers`, corporate-TLS trust via redsim's `tls_verify`, `max_retries=0`, and a usage ledger), no litellm code path even though garak installs it transitively (assert it). Run garak in a fresh credential-minimised subprocess per job (fresh `_config`, XDG dirs and garak.log in the work dir, `system.lite=False`, parallelism off, `generations=1`, prompt cap) because garak's global config, import-time logging and multiprocessing are unsafe inside a long-lived Celery parent; the child only ever holds the probe key, which comes from a project AuthProfile, not env. Datasets: none new — garak's bundled corpora are loaded by garak itself and only referenced (never copied) in the public data repo, because the in-the-wild jailbreak set has no confirmed upstream licence. Default probe set `redsim-core` is offline-detector-only (DAN/in-the-wild, prompt injection, latent injection, encoding, Do-Not-Answer, goodside/grandma/phrasing/divergence/exploitation/web_injection) so the Fargate worker, which has no HF-hub egress, never silently drops detectors; HF-backed detectors are an opt-in `redsim-extended` set with a pre-cached model dir, and HarmBench stays excluded (garak 0.16.0 reaches it only through the inactive FITD probe that needs a red-team LLM and a model-as-judge — an owner decision). The scorecard is k/n per probe family with a validator that forbids MRI/grade keys, LLM runs write no `ml_campaigns` row and are refused by `/campaign` and `/compare` (D9), findings carry a hit-rate-derived severity labelled as such, reports reuse the six-section renderer with 'prompts and responses are stored, not displayed', and a test asserts that no prompt substring from garak's own data files reaches any rendered surface. Tests run fully offline through an httpx MockTransport in-process and a loopback ThreadingHTTPServer fake gateway for the child, under a new `garak` marker executed by a separate py3.12 CI job so the unit lanes and the torch-less 3.13 lane never install garak. The two blockers are owner/ops decisions, not code: provisioning the permission-gate-only persona and key (until then runs must be declared `guardrail_mode=content_filtered` and say so), and whether HarmBench via FITD is worth two extra Pythia model roles.

## MODALITIES

MODALITIES — Phase B text/NLP classifier and object-detection modalities: bundled models and open-licensed datasets, TextFooler-style word substitution and adversarial patch/DPatch adapters, controls at the same budget, measurements with denominators, SHAP text attributions (and honest ExplainerUnavailable for detection), per-modality MRI semantics, datasets loaders, build-assets, registry entries, sandbox loading, API modality checks, e2e tests, and the P0 schema-additive changes each needs. Audited read-only on worktree /Users/noah.behrick/NDIA Hackathon/wt/planb at 10650da (2026-09-09).

**Recommendation.** Leanest honest path for MODALITIES, in this order.

1. Contracts first (S items 01–05, 44): additive literals `text`/`detection` on Domain and Modality, `edit`/`patch_area` on Norm, optional `Measurement.edit_fraction_mean` + `Measurement.detection: DetectionMetrics`, optional `Observation.text`/`Observation.detection`, optional `MLModelManifest.text`/`.detection`, and four new artifact kinds — each announced in master plan §5 per plan 01 §8, each with a round-trip test proving Phase A records and MANIFEST digests are unchanged.

2. The one refactor that unlocks both modalities (09, L): split `run_campaign` into a shared frame plus a `ModalityRunner` (classification = today's code byte-for-byte, guarded by a golden-record test; text; detection). Stages, audit vocabulary, stage_table, not_run and partial semantics stay identical so tasks/ml_campaign.py and the sandbox boundary need only env pins (10: NLTK_DATA, TORCH_HOME offline). Then the registry/admission plumbing (06–08: SUPPORTED_MODALITIES table, norm↔modality check, per-modality default grids, detection n_samples cap).

3. Text track (11–26), fully buildable now with no owner decision except the public-repo redaction question (12):
   - Dataset: UCI SMS Spam Collection — CC BY 4.0 on the UCI page (verified), 5,574 messages, 203 KB zip reachable, security-domain, tiny. AG-News, SST-2 and the HF SMS mirrors carry `license: unknown`, so they are out under §11.1.
   - Model: scikit-learn TF-IDF(1–2-gram, tokenizer identical to the SHAP masker `\W+`) + LogisticRegression as a digest-checked bundled joblib (the §9.2 bundled-sklearn path already exists). Trains in seconds on CPU; accuracy and macro-F1 recorded in MANIFEST.json only.
   - Attack: in-repo `word_substitution` (greedy leave-one-out importance + WordNet 3.0 synonyms, black-box, query-counted) labelled "TextFooler-style" with stated deviations. TextAttack is rejected (42): not installed, drags in flair/bert-score/Java `language_tool_python`/OpenHowNet, and the real TextFooler recipe needs counter-fitted embeddings and a TensorFlow-Hub Universal Sentence Encoder — impossible in a no-network sandbox child on a CPU laptop. WordNet is fetched once at build time from nltk_data on GitHub (reachable), licence recorded, stored under assets/ with a sha256; CI uses a committed tiny synonym JSON.
   - Control: random word swaps at the same edit budget; ε = share of words substituted, grid {0.1, 0.2, 0.3}, reference 0.2 — this fits the existing (0,1] validator and makes S_eps an AUC over the edit grid, comparable only with text campaigns at the same grid and lexicon (lexicon sha256 in provenance/settings). All five subscores exist (conf_gap from predict_proba; expl_shift from positional token attributions, valid because 1:1 substitution preserves token count — verified in the venv: shap 0.52 Partition + `maskers.Text(r"\W+")` explains two texts in 2.1 s), so text gets its own MRI, never mixed with image/tabular (`delta()` already refuses cross-modality).
   - Text upload stays 501 with a reason (24): no accepted format carries a tokenizer.
   - The LLM writer's text summary carries counts and ranks only — never tokens or message text (D5).

4. Detection track (27–41): technically feasible on CPU and already proven in the venv — ART 1.20.1 has `DPatch`, `RobustDPatch`, `AdversarialPatchPyTorch` and `PyTorchFasterRCNN`; torchvision 0.29 has `fasterrcnn_mobilenet_v3_large_320_fpn` (19 M params; COCO checkpoint on download.pytorch.org is reachable); DPatch ran in 0.48 s for 2 iterations on a tiny model. In-repo mAP (31) avoids the missing pycocotools/torchmetrics. Detection has no SHAP → `ExplainerUnavailable` with box evidence (35), `conf_gap` is undefined → the MRI is not computed by construction and the scorecard shows S_acc/S_asr/S_eps with denominators (36). Verify reports family deltas and Δrecall, never a ΔMRI (39).
   The dataset is the blocker (27): the owner's rawsi18 candidate is CC BY 4.0 (verified via the Kaggle metadata API) but is 4.2 GB (subset only, Kaggle token at build as for the URL data) and reportedly includes soldier/civilian/weapon classes that sit on the D3 "no targeting, no person recognition" bound. Recommend the owner approve rawsi18 with person and weapon classes excluded at build time (recorded in the manifest and every campaign's limitations); fallbacks are VisDrone2019-DET (CC BY-SA 3.0, also has pedestrian classes) and Roboflow-100 aerial-cows (CC BY 4.0, no persons, off-topic). Illia56 is Apache-2.0 but has only 40 annotated images — too thin for denominators. Do not start 27–29 before the decision is written into docs/project-brief.md.

5. Close with reports (43), datasets/capabilities routes (07, 45), docs/README honesty (46) and the CI guard (47): `nltk` joins the `ml` extra, every new test is `ml`-marked with importorskip, and the API-process no-ML probe keeps the new targets import-light.

Rough total: text ≈ 5–6 developer-days, detection ≈ 6–8 developer-days after the dataset decision, shared contracts and dispatch ≈ 3 days. Everything unimplemented at any point stays 501/ExplainerUnavailable with a reason; nothing is faked.

## ATTACKS_HARDEN

ATTACKS_HARDEN — Phase B attacks (Carlini-Wagner L2, DeepFool, image HopSkipJump, ZOO), black-box explain (KernelSHAP), hardening by training (adversarial training, defensive distillation) with derived targets and the verify loop, rule/MeasuredDelta linkage, and the spec 15 per-project weights. Audited read-only on worktree "/Users/noah.behrick/NDIA Hackathon/wt/planb" at 10650da. Evidence paths below are relative to that worktree root; line numbers are from this tree. Verified in the venv (/Users/noah.behrick/NDIA Hackathon/ndia-red-team-simulator/.venv): art 1.20.1 exposes CarliniL2Method, DeepFool, ZooAttack, HopSkipJump, AdversarialTrainer, AdversarialTrainerMadryPGD, DefensiveDistillation, BlackBoxClassifier; shap 0.52.0; torch 2.14.0; sklearn 1.9.0. Built assets on the laptop (ndia-red-team-simulator/assets/MANIFEST.json): vehicles_cnn is a resnet18 at 3x128x128, 44.8 MB, 12 epochs took 1458 s on CPU (n_train 7823); url_trees is a sklearn HistGradientBoosting (no gradients); the train splits have file=None (only eval slices are bundled).

**Recommendation.** Leanest honest path for ATTACKS_HARDEN, in dependency order. (1) Attacks first, all on ART 1.20.1 already in the venv (CarliniL2Method, DeepFool, ZooAttack, HopSkipJump verified present; no new dependency): add the optional adapter attribute `norms` plus `norm:*` capability tags and the admission/runner norm check (item 03), then three adapters cw_l2, deepfool (image, white-box, minimal-norm, L2 evaluation grid DEFAULT_EPS_GRID_L2 already in scoring.py) and zoo (tabular, score-based, BlackBoxClassifier over scaled predict_proba exactly like the existing HopSkipJump tabular path, frozen features re-imposed post hoc because ZooAttack has no mask) (items 01, 02, 05); fix `GET /v1/attacks?modality=` to filter by capability tags so image HopSkipJump is listed, and give HopSkipJump image-domain defaults measured on the real resnet18@128 (items 04, 22). The eps/pert/queries machinery for minimal-norm attacks already exists in campaign.py and is inherited unchanged (item 06). (2) Explain: no new library; KernelExplainer for predict-only tabular targets already exists as the fallback and only needs a black-box double, the spec's 100-row background and an explicit `explainer` choice (items 07, 09); KernelSHAP on 3x128x128 images is infeasible on CPU and the spec's own PartitionExplainer fallback is built and tested, so record that decision rather than build a path that cannot run (item 08). (3) Harden: keep `defense:<id>` as the one reference prefix and add `kind: training` rows to the defenses catalog (item 10); bundle a capped stratified training slice of the SAME MIT-licensed military_vehicles dataset in build-assets so the network-less sandbox child has training data (no new dataset, so nothing is added to the public data repo; item 11); implement adversarial fine-tuning with ART AdversarialTrainer (PGD/FGSM at the campaign's reference eps, bounded epochs/train_n/wall budget, backbone frozen by default) and a native torch defensive distillation (ART's DefensiveDistillation requires probability outputs and has no temperature, verified in source) as the `defense_apply` stage INSIDE the existing verify.replay child (items 12, 14). That keeps spec 15.6 intact without an owner decision: the base model_sha256 and settings_hash stay equal, the defense is the single changed variable, and the derived weights travel in Provenance.defense; the worker then registers the derived state_dict as a new Target with `derived_from` lineage through the standard model.validate path (item 13) and the report/compare/audit surfaces name the derived digest and training budget (item 18). Tree-ensemble targets get an explicit 422 with the reason, never a fake (item 20). (4) Per-project weights are one additive migration (projects.ml_scoring), a field on the existing PUT /projects/{slug}/settings, and admission precedence; comparability is already enforced because scoring is in settings_hash (item 19). (5) All schema changes are additive and default-valued (DerivedFrom, STAGES defense_apply, CampaignConfig.explainer, two artifact kinds) and announced per plan 01 section 8 (item 15); tests follow the existing ml/unit split so the torch-less 3.13 lane stays green (item 23). Sizing: attacks S+M+S+S+M, explain M+S+S, harden M+M+L+M+M+S+S+S, weights M, docs S. Measure the CPU budgets on the real bundled models before fixing defaults (item 22); the sandbox wall clock (1200 s) is the binding constraint, and any overrun is already surfaced as SandboxTimeout/partial rather than hidden.

**Recommendation.** Leanest honest path for REVIEW_REPORTS, in merge order. (1) One schema-additive PR announced in master plan section 5: items 01, 08, 18 (review vocabulary + history/revisions, retest links, `CampaignRecord.schema_version`); all default-valued so every Phase A row and the frozen fixture validate unchanged. (2) One shared migration `0011_phase_b_platform` (item 44) carrying `report_snapshots`, `idempotency_keys` and `projects.ml_scoring` with 0010's RLS/trigger parity, so the frozen head moves once; update the heads test. (3) Review workflow as a new `redsim/services/finding_review.py` generalising the existing `review_finding` into a transition table (items 02, 03, 04, 06, 07, 11, 12) — confirm/reopen/resolve/request_changes never invent a `Finding.status` value: `confirmed` keeps `open`, `resolved` sits on top of the worker-written `fixed`/`poc_passed` and is refused with 409 `resolution_blocked` listing unmet conditions (poc_passed, equal settings_hash, independent reviewer). Analyst-authored drafts (item 05) are the only L-sized piece and add `Action.FINDING_AUTHOR`; schedule it last in this track. (4) PDF export: reportlab >= 4.2 (pure Python, py3-none-any, no system libraries, works on the python:3.14-slim worker with no egress and in both CI unit lanes without the `ml` marker), bundled DejaVu TTFs for ε/Δ/≥, `invariant=1` for byte-identical output, `pypdf` in the test extra for assertions (items 15-17). Rejected: weasyprint (pango/cairo apt packages in the slim image and CI), Playwright/Chromium (Node plus ~300 MB browser in a worker image that has neither; `@playwright/test` is not even installed in web), xhtml2pdf kept only as a lower-effort fallback if the team accepts the pyHanko dependency chain and unverified 3.14 support. The PDF is a third projection of `CampaignRecord`, never a recomputation, and must carry the six 14.8 sections, denominators, GRADE_STATEMENT and limitations. (5) Snapshots = one immutable `report_snapshots` row per render pointing at the existing content-addressed Artifact rows, a `report.render` admission route (audit-first, copy `create_finding_action_job`), list/versioned fetch and admin archive/restore as a soft flag (items 20-22); if the owner wants to skip the table, the `report.render` Job row can stand in as the snapshot — record the choice. (6) N-run comparison as a pure `redsim/ml/compare.py` reused by the pairwise route and the retest listing (items 26, 09): request-order rows, deltas only for verify rows against their own baseline, no mean/rank column (D9 i), 409 on any incompatible pair or partial score. (7) Per-project weights stored on `projects.ml_scoring`, validated as a full five-vector through `ScoringConfig` (never filled or renormalised), copied into `config.scoring` at admission so `settings_hash` splits comparability honestly, with a non-default-weights badge everywhere the score renders (items 28-30). (8) `Idempotency-Key` as a FastAPI dependency backed by the new table, applied to every admission POST, replaying the stored 202 and refusing body mismatch with 422 (items 31-32); record the key digest as `request_identity` in the run snapshot and audit detail (F004 FR-003). (9) F008 platform items that are buildable now: `policy.deny` chain events (38), the audit browse endpoint with cursor pagination (39), and API-side audit export reusing `canonical_jsonl`/WORM helpers (40); plus the writer detail bound (43). Blocked and to be put to the owner rather than coded: pickle trust override (33; spec conflicts approver vs admin — recommend declining), in-app membership editor and invitations (35, 36; contradict canonical 7.8 "roles are claims"), non-ranked reviewer role (13), and everything behind D006 (41, 42). Infeasible on the current toolchain: TensorFlow SavedModel (34; no CPython 3.14 wheels, code-executing format) — ship the tf2onnx hint instead. Libraries: reportlab, pillow (worker extra), pypdf (test extra), DejaVu fonts with licence; no new ML libraries. Datasets: none — this scope adds no data; the open/unclassified/licensed rule and the public data repo are untouched, and no fixture is ever served as a result. CI stays green because every new test is pure Python (no `ml` marker) and runs in both the 3.12 and torch-less 3.13 lanes; Postgres-only assertions (row locks, RLS, alembic round-trip) stay in the integration lane.

## INTEROP

INTEROP — Phase B2 interoperability (spec 27 in full, 17.4, 19 F002 consume / F007 export, 21.8, 25, 23 row B2; docs/plans/07 for the parallel-execution shape only). Audited read-only on worktree "/Users/noah.behrick/NDIA Hackathon/wt/planb" at 10650da (feat(infra): deploy public HTTPS Fargate demo runtime (#23)). Covers: Croissant/Parquet adversarial-dataset export of a run, POST /v1/runs/{id}/dataset and GET /v1/datasets/{id}, the consume side (ONNX models via the existing upload path; Croissant/Parquet evaluation slices via POST /v1/datasets under the licence rule), MITRE ATLAS tags at Finding.schema_blob.ml.atlas_technique plus a per-campaign coverage route, env-selected Palantir Foundry push (off by default, REST, no standing credentials, no LLM), the Anduril Lattice push (text-only, blocked under D3), audit events, RBAC, tests with a fake Foundry endpoint. Web UI (web/, packages/) excluded: API contracts only.

**Recommendation.** Leanest honest path for INTEROP, in the order the dependencies force and as three parallel writers with disjoint files (the only thing docs/plans/07 is still good for):

Wave 0 (one small PR, same day): INTEROP-01 stubs (the three 17.4 routes answer 501 after gates — today they 404 while docs/api/v1.md claims 501), INTEROP-02 Action members mirrored in rego/cedar, INTEROP-03 error codes (and move models.py's local LICENSE_REQUIRED into the table), INTEROP-28 audit vocabulary + JWT-shaped token redaction, INTEROP-29 env/capabilities block, INTEROP-35 the plan-01 §8 announcement that B2 needs no redsim/ml/schema.py change. After this the tree is truthful and every later item replaces one stub.

Writer A — contribute (export): INTEROP-04 (additive per-sample keys in adv_slice + clean_slice.npz + control slices; the one seam plan 07 §3 warned about), then INTEROP-05/06/07 (pure Croissant builder over pyarrow, projection-equality guard, regenerate-in-child mode), then INTEROP-08/09/10/11/12 (dataset.export task on the scans queue writing datasets/<run-id>/ into the artifacts bucket by default, card template, admission on a follow-up Run so rollup_run_status never reopens a terminal campaign, GET manifest as application/ld+json, one-export-per-run idempotency).

Writer B — consume: INTEROP-14 (migration 0011 ml_datasets with the 0010 RLS SQL copied), INTEROP-13 (POST /v1/datasets static checks: PAR1 magic, manifest shape, remote contentUrl refused, licence required, schema declared, audit before rows), INTEROP-15 (parse only in the sandbox child), INTEROP-16 (bind a consumed slice to uploads/campaigns; D9(i) falls out of settings_hash). INTEROP-17 (ONNX from other teams) is already done by the Phase A upload path — docs only.

Writer C — ATLAS + Foundry: INTEROP-19 pin the exact ATLAS release (github mitre-atlas/atlas-data is reachable), INTEROP-18 stamp atlas_technique in build_finding_detail (one line; schema already frozen-in), INTEROP-20 expose ATLAS on GET /v1/attacks from the registry constant (do not add the AttackInfo field), INTEROP-21 coverage route with no numeric field, INTEROP-22 report line; then INTEROP-23/24/25 Foundry client + payload validator (D9 guard), integration.push job on the default pool with an admin-gated route, and the fake-endpoint tests with httpx.MockTransport.

Then INTEROP-31 invariants (no key/model/URL string leaves; no bare MRI; ml-marked so the 3.13 lane stays green), INTEROP-32 docs, INTEROP-33 publish one tabular sample export to IntelliBridge/ai-red-teaming-data as the contribute proof.

Owner decisions to request now, not after code: INTEROP-26 (procure a Foundry tenant or record the exit check as unmet), INTEROP-27 (Lattice stays text-only under D3 — no code, capability status line only), INTEROP-30 (approve the datasets/ S3 prefix map and a Foundry egress CIDR variable with William), INTEROP-34 (D006 for imagery redistribution; tabular exports are unblocked).

Libraries: pyarrow (already in the ml extra, 25.0.1 in the venv) for Parquet with deterministic writer settings; a hand-rolled Croissant 1.0 JSON-LD builder plus an in-tree structural validator and jsonschema (present) — mlcroissant only as an optional test dependency behind importorskip because it is absent from the venv and drags a heavy tree; httpx (already in api/ml extras) for Foundry with the tests/test_llm_pythia.py MockTransport pattern (respx is absent); moto (test extra) for the export-bucket tests (tests/test_worm_export.py pattern); boto3 default credential chain (task role), never static keys. No new runtime dependency. Datasets: exports derive only from the bundled, licensed datasets (CC0 URL feature vectors publishable; MIT-tagged vehicle imagery blocked for redistribution under D006); consumed slices must carry a licence statement and, by the owner rule, be published to the public data repo by the operator before any demo use. CPU-only and laptop-sized throughout: nothing here trains a model.

**Recommendation.** Leanest honest path (all repo work is credential-free; every apply step is William's with AWS_PROFILE=MIL, listed per item): 1) INFRA-03 first — make upload_assets.py manifest-driven (exclude fixture_only CIFAR-10, the stale bundled/url_classifier dir and eval.csv), test it, then William uploads the existing laptop-built tree (vehicles_cnn resnet18 0.7687 / url_trees 0.9087, both from open MIT/CC0 datasets already recorded in the public data repo — no new dataset, nothing fixture served) and pins asset_bundle. 2) INFRA-04/06/07 together as one runtime PR: the storage-prefix contract test + tfvars for project `demo`, a `tenant` one-off task (api image) and a `seed` one-off task (worker image, `redsim ml seed`) — these reuse the existing register_bundled_model service and run_task.py pattern; no new libraries (boto3 only, via `uv run --no-project --with boto3`). 3) INFRA-05 flips enable_workers after the seed task proves the bundle loads on the Fargate image (guards the torch/sklearn version-skew risk). 4) INFRA-08/09 as one realm PR: realm.json gains the `redsim-smoke` client and an audience mapper (the API rejects bearers without aud `redsim`, auth.py:78-87), plus `configure_realm.py` (httpx against Keycloak admin REST, already a dependency) that fixes the live realm's missing mapper and creates the four demo users with passwords stored only in Secrets Manager. 5) INFRA-10 live smoke (stdlib, `live` marker, skips without REDSIM_LIVE_URL) — its unauthenticated tier already passes against the live URL today; the campaign tier runs only with an explicit flag. 6) INFRA-17 + INFRA-11: describe_runtime.py (digest resolution and evidence) then the deploy-job rewrite (register digest-pinned revisions, migration-before-rollout, no identity auto-roll, ignore_changes on task_definition) with the deploy-role IAM extension and ECS_* repo variables set only after it merges; until then rollouts stay Terraform-driven and manual, which is correct. 7) INFRA-12 adds the credential-free infra-checks workflow (terraform 1.16.1 via setup-terraform; local terraform is 1.15.8 so use tfenv) — cheap and it protects everything above. 8) INFRA-13 (WORM) and INFRA-14 (Pythia egress) are blocked on owner decisions with real cost/irreversibility consequences: recommend GOVERNANCE mode with 30-day retention for the demo audit bucket (COMPLIANCE/2555 days is irreversible and undeletable), and keep the deployed demo rules-only (`narrative_source="rules"`, already honest in the UI) unless the owner accepts a ~$33/month NAT gateway for the `default` pool; Terraform for both is code-and-plan and can be written behind toggles now. 9) INFRA-15/16/20/21/22 are documentation and evidence: run the first-run measurement locally on this laptop (no AWS needed) and record it in the README, add the cost/teardown page, refresh the stale README/deploy.md/ci.md statements (the OIDC failure is fixed; deploy-aws runs green with the deploy job skipped), and write the single ordered activation runbook in the same PRs as the scripts. Skip INFRA-18 (log-ingest) — the spec marks it optional and CloudWatch already holds the logs. No schema changes are needed anywhere in this scope, so the redsim/ml/schema.py freeze is untouched; CI stays green because every new test is either a Terraform mock plan, a unittest of a script, or a `live`-marked test that skips without REDSIM_LIVE_URL.

**Recommendation.** Leanest honest path, in order: (1) one migration 0011 doing everything additive at once — `ml_campaigns.batch_id` (indexed), a small `ml_batches` table with 0010's RLS parity copied verbatim, `idempotency_key`/`request_sha256` on it, and two nullable `projects` columns for the ML run cap and daily run budget — announced in master plan §5 per plan-01 §8; do NOT touch the frozen redsim/ml/schema.py (overlay batch_id onto the /campaign response the way reviewer_notes already is; BULK-02 records the optional additive fields if the owner insists). (2) One service module redsim/services/ml_batches.py that reuses the existing admission boundaries rather than reimplementing them: factor create_attack_campaign's validation into a write-free `admit_attack_config`, add a `batch_id` kwarg to create_attack_campaign and create_verify_campaign, and keep exactly one audit row per run plus one `batch.create`/`batch.cancel`/`bulk.upload` row per batch — the vocabulary and codes added once in errors.py and the spec's §17.3/§5.11 tables (the error-code test parses the spec, so the spec edit is mandatory, not optional). (3) One router redsim/api/v1/batches.py (POST/GET/GET list/compare/cancel) with the attacks.py gate order and lazy service imports so the API process stays ML-import-free; batch compare generalises compare.py's side_by_side to N scorecards grouped by comparability and never emits a delta, mean, rank or cross-modality anything (a batch is one modality by construction, refused with `batch_modality_mismatch` + groups). (4) Capacity as data, not broker introspection: per-project caps on Project, deferral (queued + `deferred`) instead of refusal for concurrency, a daily-admission budget that refuses 429 with an audited row, a continuation dispatch hook at the end of ml_campaign_run plus a 60 s beat backstop, gauges filled by the same sampler (the existing `redsim_jobs_active` gauge is defined and never set today), and `GET /v1/ml/capacity` for the numbers. (5) Bulk upload by extracting the inline single-upload admission into a service function first (BULK-12), then a `/v1/models/bulk` multipart+manifest route with a total-bytes and file-count cap; every file keeps its own Target, ml.ingest run, model.validate job and model.register row; nothing is deserialised in the API. (6) Bulk verify as N linked verify runs under a verify batch (as asked), but get the owner's decision on BULK-16 first: N findings of one run produce N identical defended measurement sets, and one defended run projected onto each finding from its own attack rows is equally honest and N times cheaper on a laptop CPU. (7) CLI: multi-target positional and a pure-Python YAML matrix expander (PyYAML is already a dependency) driving the unchanged `run_offline_campaign`, one audit.jsonl chain per run, a summary table and summary.json; `--api` mode maps modality groups onto the batch route through ApiClient. Libraries: none new (FastAPI, SQLAlchemy/Alembic, Celery, pydantic, PyYAML, prometheus_client already present; torch.onnx.export only inside tests). Datasets: none new — the e2e uses the harness's synthetic tiny image set and the committed malicious-URLs sample, and the bulk-upload e2e exports a tiny ONNX from the tiny bundled weights; no fixture is ever served as a result and nothing is added to the public data repo. Owner decisions needed before code: BULK-16 (verify dedupe) and whether batch_id enters the frozen evidence schema (BULK-02; recommend no). Web UI stays deferred: every route here is API-only and documented with its 17.3 envelope.

**Recommendation.** Leanest honest path for TESTS_DOCS: (1) land the tripwires first, before any Phase B code merges: TESTS_DOCS-40 (schema-compat test on the frozen P0 fixture), TESTS_DOCS-17 (blocked-module and child-env lists extended), TESTS_DOCS-01 (the `garak` marker) and TESTS_DOCS-04 (a CI job that finally runs the existing Python e2e tier, which passes 8/8 on 10650da but is run by no workflow today). (2) Build every Phase B test double as a loopback stdlib `http.server` thread plus an `httpx.MockTransport` twin, reusing the `PythiaToggle` pattern already in tests/e2e/harness.py: `TinyEndpointServer` (classifier /predict), `FakeOpenAIChatServer` (so garak's real `openai.OpenAICompatible` generator is exercised with `uri` pointed at the fake, exactly the D6 shape it will use for `{PYTHIA_BASE_URL}/v1/`), `FakeFoundryServer`; keep TinyTextTarget and TinyDetector at 8x8/16x16 with 12-16 samples so the whole e2e tier stays under 10 minutes on a laptop CPU. Libraries: garak pinned to 0.16.0 (verified to run fully offline with `test.Blank` in 2 s; drive it through `--config` YAML because `--model_type/--probes` are deprecated in 0.16), ART's `PyTorchObjectDetector` for detection, `pyarrow` (already in ml) for Parquet and `mlcroissant` only as a test-extra consumer check, a pure-Python PDF renderer (fpdf2 or reportlab) so the worker image and SAST lanes stay stable; nothing new in the API image. Datasets: no new image or tabular data; the text and detection datasets are chosen by their scopes and every one goes into IntelliBridge/ai-red-teaming-data with an INDEX.csv row and licence, cross-checked by a vendored INDEX snapshot in tests (no network in tests). (3) Documentation is one rewrite pass, not incremental patching: docs/api/v1.md regenerated from the OpenAPI document with a Phase B status table kept honest by a `tests/test_docs_phase_b_consistency.py` gate, README gaining an explicit "Open items and not implemented" section that shrinks to the web UI plus the owner decisions, plan 07 rewritten onto the section 27 vocabulary after the INTEROP scope fixes module names, master plan bumped to v2.4 with the Phase B waves and the schema-additive announcements, and register 10 consolidating all scopes. (4) Two items need the owner before anything is written: the public data repo already publishes garak's `data/` directory at head 754fbca with an unconfirmed upstream licence on the in-the-wild jailbreak corpus, contradicting spec 11.6's "redistributes none" (TESTS_DOCS-33), and Lattice stays text-only under the D3 bound (TESTS_DOCS-41). (5) The Phase B definition of done is one script, `make check-phase-b` (TESTS_DOCS-36), that runs ruff, mypy, the default, ml, garak and e2e tiers, `mkdocs build --strict`, the docs-consistency test and the live HTTP probes, and that provably fails on today's main at the garak and capabilities steps.
